14:T5206,## Problem

Eliza's coding-agent stack is intended to be configurable to the same direct coding loop as Pi while retaining durable projects, tasks, subagents, services, evaluators, and secure user interaction. A pinned, trace-backed comparison against Pi and Codex found several correctness and efficiency gaps.

Baseline: origin/develop at ffbe17703d1f02c54e128b212b74a35686bbf278.
Pi baseline: earendil-works/pi at c49906ec77788625aacbdc53ebca6fbe65bd20f5.
Benchmark: 2 of 731 checked-in SWE-bench Pro helper tasks (0.27%), with official and behavior-level judges kept separate.

## Confirmed defects

1. OpenAI-compatible coding turns fail before the first model call. Native tools are wrapped with the AI SDK jsonSchema helper, which exposes an enumerable accessor; deepToWellFormedUnicode then rejects that wrapper as unsafe.
2. The one-shot code CLI reports the resulting synthetic runtime failure as success: true and exits zero.
3. Coding mode always pays a Stage 1 HANDLE_RESPONSE model boundary before the planner, unlike Pi's direct tool loop.
4. Coding-only budget environment values are parsed even when coding mode is disabled.
5. FILE read rejects files above 262144 bytes before applying offset/limit, so documented chunking cannot read large files.
6. SHELL_HISTORY renders unbounded historical stdout/stderr into context.
7. Credential redemption checks child activity before polling but not again before returning a secret.
8. Verifier infrastructure/model outages are treated as worker acceptance failures and can consume a corrective attempt.
9. Automatic verifier exceptions can leave tasks in validating; tasks without criteria can also remain there.
10. Benchmark/result surfaces omit model calls, token/cache/cost data, backend/model identity, and trajectory correlation.
11. ACP and native child result surfaces are asymmetric and do not share a canonical typed terminal result envelope.

## Trace-backed baseline

- Eliza: zero LLM calls; trajectory status errored at tool-schema normalization; CLI still returned success.
- Pi, task 1: 11 calls, 63641 input plus cache-read tokens, 75.0% aggregate cache hit; behavior judge passed.
- Pi, task 2: 11 calls, 76848 input plus cache-read tokens, 69.6% aggregate cache hit; source was left syntactically invalid and the final answer admitted failure.
- Codex, task 1: 11 generations, 382739 input tokens, 346368 cached (90.5%); behavior judge passed.
- Codex, task 2: 29 generations, 1316555 input tokens, 1238784 cached (94.1%); behavior judge passed.
- Claude: local CLI is installed but unauthenticated, so no valid run has been claimed.

The upstream hidden patches for both selected tasks have fixture defects, so official grader failures and behavior verdicts are recorded separately.

## Proposed delivery

### P0 correctness

- Sanitize raw tool schemas before constructing AI SDK wrappers; never deep-walk accessor-bearing transport wrappers.
- Add a live RESPONSE_HANDLER regression that proves a native tool reaches the provider boundary.
- Propagate synthetic runtime failures through AgentClient, CLI, and ACP as typed non-success terminal states.
- Recheck child/session authorization immediately before credential return.
- Represent verifier infrastructure failure as inconclusive/retryable, not worker failure; do not consume corrective attempts.
- Guarantee every validating task reaches a typed terminal or retry state.

### P1 coding-loop parity

- Add a per-session direct coding loop that avoids the mandatory HANDLE_RESPONSE preflight while preserving the existing general conversational path.
- Move coding budgets/configuration from process-global environment mutation to session-scoped configuration.
- Make FILE offset/limit true bounded reads for large files.
- Preserve complete shell history when selected for model context. If the final provider-specific request cannot admit it, reject before dispatch rather than slicing history. Bounded reads use only explicit caller-requested pagination with exact continuation, never an implicit recent window.
- Preserve stable prompt/tool prefixes and expose exact per-call usage/cache/context metrics.

### P1 orchestration and ACP

- Use one canonical child result envelope across Eliza, Pi ACP, Claude ACP, and Codex ACP.
- Use typed states: complete, failed, timed out, cancelled, blocked, awaiting question, awaiting credential, awaiting approval, and verifier-inconclusive.
- Store full canonical results while truncating only UI projections.
- Correlate parent trajectory, child run, ACP events, harness-native trace, tool calls, usage, and verification under one run ID.
- Separate execution status from result-delivery status and test restart/idempotency/orphan recovery.

## Acceptance criteria

- The live Cerebras/OpenAI-compatible coding path reaches the model with native tools and records a non-empty trajectory.
- Synthetic runtime failures exit nonzero and cannot be labeled successful.
- Coding mode can complete a tool loop without an unconditional response-handler call.
- Large files can be read incrementally with bounded output.
- Shell history never uses an implicit context cap or recent window; complete selected history is passed unchanged or the exact final request is rejected before dispatch. Explicit pagination is caller-requested and losslessly continuable.
- Credentials cannot be redeemed after a child becomes terminal.
- Verifier outages do not fail worker work or spend correction attempts.
- Every validation path has a deterministic terminal/retry transition.
- The lightweight benchmark records full logical trajectories and normalized calls, tokens, cache reads/writes, context growth, tools, wall time, patch, tests, and final-state truthfulness.
- Package tests/typechecks/lint and root verification pass at the final SHA, with pre-existing exact-head failures explicitly separated.

## Critical implementation audit — 2026-09-06

Audited source: `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`. This update separates the original coding epic from the **three-day Sprint 2 general single-request completion slice**. No live benchmark, acceptance run or new implementation is claimed.

### Existing foundation and historical corrections

- Live GitHub inspection confirms #24526, #28083 and #28094 are merged. The body’s original zero-model-call/CLI-success baseline is historical; do not reland the replacement coding lane, parent-broker work or diagnostic repair implementation. Current [coding verification handling](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts#L4749) and [typed repair exhaustion](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts#L5217) contain the newer behavior. The last historical comment also records Claude authentication and a two-task run; treating Claude as permanently unauthenticated would be stale.
- Existing [multi-step loop regression](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/planner-loop-8007-multistep.test.ts#L1) covers prior tool results reaching later planner calls and duplicate successful-call suppression; [advance regression](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/planner-loop-multistep-advance.test.ts#L1) exercises sequential orchestration with scripted models/results. These are valuable mechanics tests, but neither proves a live user request completes eight real dependent operations.
- The named [live scenario](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/test/scenarios/cross-cutting/cross.multi-action-chain.create-todo-then-block-sites.scenario.ts#L38) explicitly uses **two user turns**. It checks a todo count delta and site names in an action result. It does not prove one-turn clause completeness, a calendar write, a view receipt or ordering of final delivery. This is a confirmed evidence gap, not proof that every multi-step production request fails.

### Bounded Sprint 2 implementation plan

1. **Pin a reproducible one-request baseline.** Extend the real cross-cutting scenario path with one user message requesting todo creation, a calendar block and opening calendar. Record starting database/calendar/view state, exact runtime/model/settings and all Stage-1/planner/action/delivery events. Keep the existing two-turn scenario as a different contract. Resolve actual registered action/subaction names at the current revision rather than assuming old CREATE_TASK/LIFE naming defines success.
2. **Assert all clauses through effects.** Validate the persisted todo and returned ID, persisted calendar event with correct date/time/timezone and relationship where supported, and acknowledged active calendar view. Require matching receipts for the requested target/operation before the final completion statement, using #17027's contract. A tool invocation, model-generated success string or action-name match does not count. #17030 supplies the stage record and #29670 the navigation seam; do not create duplicate receipt formats.
3. **Reproduce before patching the loop.** Inspect the first missing clause/incorrect terminal transition. Follow candidate routing, tool results, next-step planner history, evaluator CONTINUE/FINISH and action-owned response flags. Fix the owning transition only if the baseline demonstrates premature finish, discarded result or duplicate execution. Retain current general planner and coding-specific guards; never force arbitrary tool counts or unconditional retries to make the test pass. Preserve complete context/tool history or return the explicit resource-limit error before dispatch.
4. **Add the eight-step dependent-ID case.** Use real task/calendar/domain records whose later operations consume earlier returned identifiers, with observable final state for every step and a single initial authorization. Include interleaved reads and writes and verify no extra user prompt is needed just to continue already authorized work. Make the case reproducible with isolated entities/rooms and deterministic cleanup, while retaining live model decisions.
5. **Exercise uncertainty and interruption.** Add transient failure before a mutation, ambiguous transport failure after a mutation, persistent failure after earlier success, missing required input, permission-required operation, explicit cancellation and resource exhaustion. Retry only when the domain supports idempotency or reconciliation; read the actual write receipt/state before retrying an ambiguous result. Preserve completed effects and report remaining/blocked work truthfully. Do not silently undo completed mutations or charge infrastructure errors as user-work failure.
6. **Validate and report.** Extend focused planner regressions for each reproduced fix; run the real changed scenarios and inspect domain rows, calendar/navigation receipts, final output and complete trajectories. Correlate per-call input/output/cache tokens and TTFT with #17072, keeping substantive completion separate from early acknowledgement. Run owning package tests/typecheck/lint, scenario validation/typecheck, root verify and app audit if shared UI/app changes. Publish exact-source before/after evidence and a verified bundle, with unavailable metrics explicit.

### Broader epic remains separate

The original epic still needs a current acceptance ledger for provider wire/native tools, truthful CLI/ACP terminals, session-scoped coding configuration, large-file explicit pagination, complete shell-output/history continuity, child terminal envelopes/restart delivery, authorization lifecycle, verifier retry state and a larger pinned cross-agent benchmark. Merged implementation and old focused pass counts do not certify all current targets. Revalidate each consumer boundary on current source before opening repair work; keep any sensitive authorization finding in the private security workflow. The two-task historic comparison is diagnostic, not statistical parity or current model ranking.

Estimate boundary: three days covers the one-request baseline, narrowly reproduced general-loop fix and named behavioral proof. It does not cover ACP redesign, all coding/verifier acceptance, expanded cross-agent benchmarking, or unresolved calendar/provider permissions. If prerequisite receipt/navigation contracts are missing, record that dependency and replan; do not close the broad epic when this sprint slice passes.

<!-- guardian-project19-adversarial-2026-09-06:start -->
## Independent deep review: #24299 — general single-request slice

Audited `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`, live issue/comments and current planner/owner-action contracts. Keep the three-day Sprint 2 slice separate from the historical coding/ACP epic. This review found a specific general-loop defect to investigate before merely increasing the number of steps in the scenario.

### P1 — Successful reads are incorrectly considered immutable throughout a general turn (reproduced)

[partitionRedundantSucceededCalls](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts#L4938-L4994) treats every prior successful name+argument identity as settled for the whole trajectory. Only coding-mode `WRITE`/`EDIT` clears earlier successful identities. General domain mutations do not invalidate earlier reads. The [caller](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts#L1521-L1557) explicitly tells the model an identical successful call cannot return new information, then pushes it toward final synthesis.

Executed `/tmp/guardian-project19-deep-review/probe-24299.ts` with the source-extracted unchanged partition function and canonical serializer:

- `OWNER_TODOS(action:review, kind:definition)` succeeds.
- `OWNER_TODOS(action:create, kind:definition, title:Buy milk)` succeeds.
- The same review is classified `redundant`, with **zero fresh calls**.
- Negative control: coding `READ → EDIT → READ` correctly allows the last read.

The [current owner surface](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/owner-surfaces.ts#L142-L211) really exposes review/create through one action and [pins definition ownership](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/owner-surfaces.ts#L253-L270). These are current operation shapes, not the historical LIFE action assumed by older scenarios. The probe establishes planner filtering; it does not execute a real todo database or live model.

Independent caller qualification: owner results can carry `turnComplete:true` through [completeLifeOpsEffect](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/action-effect-result.ts#L82-L112), and the [completion gate](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts#L6460-L6512) may finish after the first read. The real regression must explicitly declare authorized remaining work (`completed:false` / `eliza_turn_scope:more_work_pending` as supported), obtain evaluator CONTINUE, and verify an actually committed create/update receipt; `success:true` alone may describe a preview. The isolated partition probe does not establish that the listed owner sequence executes end-to-end without those prerequisites.

Amend the one-request workflow to include a read, a modifying operation, and the **same read with identical arguments** to verify the effect. Give reuse a typed contract: mutating operation idempotency and read freshness/dependency revision are different. Preserve duplicate successful-write suppression; do not indiscriminately clear all identities after any tool, which would reopen repeated side effects. A conservative read invalidation after relevant writes is safer than declaring all successful reads permanently immutable. Account for external state changing while a turn waits, not only local writes. Test the final reply against actual post-write domain state.

### P2 — The duplicate-success guard does not cover duplicate calls within one model response (reproduced at partition boundary)

The same probe supplies two identical create calls with distinct call IDs and an empty prior trajectory: both appear in `fresh`. The function builds the succeeded set only from past steps, not earlier candidates in the current batch. [Enqueue/dequeue](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts#L1665-L1727) stores the allowed batch and later executes queued calls; [executeQueuedToolCall](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts#L3322-L3390) does not rerun this partition after the first succeeds. Thus the planner guard by itself cannot certify the original plan's no-duplicate-write acceptance. Whether a particular domain collapses the duplicate remains its own contract; no duplicate database row is claimed here.

Add a single model response containing repeated mutation proposals to the behavioral tests, in addition to retries across model rounds. At dispatch, bind effects to a stable authorized operation identity and reconcile domain receipts. Preserve deliberate multiplicity (“create two identical reminders”) through explicit distinct operation identities rather than treating textual argument equality as universal idempotency. Include ambiguous transport failure after the first effect and process restart; the in-memory trajectory is not durable exactly-once proof.

### Examined/disconfirmed boundaries and amended acceptance

The current loop already validates unavailable tools, keeps successful results in native assistant/tool history, drains the coding queue separately, and has typed trajectory limits. The historical zero-call provider-wrapper failure and CLI success report remain historical merged work; this review does not resurrect them. Current archived steps are included in duplicate filtering and tool-call counts, so loading an archived trajectory does not reset those guards.

Retain the first audit's real one-message todo/calendar/view scenario and complete eight-step dependent-ID case, but add the read-after-write and same-response duplicate cases above. Follow all actual receipt/result fields into the evaluator and final reply; merely counting eight calls can both miss this defect and reward redundant work. Distinguish already completed clauses, blocked clauses, retryable infrastructure failure, and ambiguous effect acceptance. Do not broaden this sprint into ACP or verifier redesign unless a reproduced failure of the chosen general workflow requires that dependency.

## Dependency update — 2026-09-06

#29958 is merged and supplies the exact R2/S3-compatible multipart object primitive with bounded parts and response-loss handling. Reuse that primitive; do not reimplement it. ## Problem

The original gap was a full-backup publication path capped at 32 MiB per immutable object without a durable multipart lifecycle. The exact multipart provider primitive has since merged in #29958; connecting it to durable publication and fulfilling the incremental-chain requirements below remains outstanding. No human decision has de-scoped incremental capture, compaction, or large-object publication, so the scale boundary is unresolved rather than intentionally limited.

This issue tracks the implementation path. It does **not** authorize deployment, staging/production mutation, provider resource creation, or enabling backup gates.

## Required invariants

- Periodic immutable full checkpoints plus real deltas bound to an exact parent/base operation.
- Bounded chain depth, reconstructed bytes and restore time; no unbounded replay.
- Deterministic compaction into a new verified full checkpoint before the bound is crossed.
- Restore reconstructs full + ordered deltas as a bounded stream and verifies every content/ciphertext hash.
- Durable multipart upload authority: upload intent, provider upload identity, exact ordered parts and checksums, completion receipt, response-loss reconciliation, abort receipt, and GC.
- Endpoint/account/bucket fingerprints remain exact; object keys and credentials never enter logs or argv.
- Retries are idempotent and cannot complete a changed body, duplicate a part, or adopt another worker's upload.
- Limits are enforced before capture/publication can exceed them; never fail only after accumulating the full body.
- Existing single-PUT objects remain readable and GC-safe.
- All gates remain literal 0 until exact-head review and staging evidence.

- [ ] Streaming delta capture with periodic full-checkpoint policy
- [ ] Chain-aware streaming restore and compaction
- [ ] Provider-neutral durable multipart state/repository
- [ ] R2 and S3-compatible multipart executors with response-loss reconciliation
- [ ] Multipart-aware replication, abort and locator-aware GC
- [ ] Scale harness at 10 MiB, 128 MiB, 1 GiB and configured maximum

Crash/restart before upload creation, after creation response loss, between every part, after part response loss, before/after complete, abort response loss, concurrent claims, endpoint repoint, changed body/checksum, out-of-order/duplicate part, stale lease, parent deletion race, chain-depth exhaustion, compaction crash, primary-to-secondary replication retry, and GC of incomplete uploads.

1c:T5627,cc @lalalune

Big one — the whole coding-agent orchestration arc, net against develop. Sectioned so it can be read (or split) by theme.

## Verification / evidence
- **eliza-code ACP tool ledger** (`packages/examples/code/src/acp-tool-ledger.ts`): the in-process eliza-code child mirrored **no** tool events, so the write ledger was empty, the deterministic pre-pass starved, and the judge saw prose only ("no execution logs" → a coaching lap on nearly every build). Completed FILE/SHELL actions now become ACP `tool_call` updates — the same path claude/codex adapters use.
- **Fabricated-input guard** (`services/fabricated-input.ts`): under verify coaching a child asked to read `/etc/<missing>.yaml` wrote its own stand-in with `version: 1.2.3`, re-pointed the script, and the judge shipped "1.2.3" to the user. The task's read targets are compared with the session's write ledger + shell redirections by file stem → immediate honest park (never coached, never passed). Judge hard rule, coaching integrity clause, and script guidance added.
- Served-page URL evidence: the router stamps `subAgentVerifiedUrls` on the live session and the evidence bundle probes the route/deploy-config URL for any verified deliverable files (was only reachable through the ledger-less branch).
- Successors inherit the predecessor's title/label/acceptance criteria + `parentTaskId` lineage; the fs-evidence floor follows lineage; deterministic TASKS send for run-it follow-ups; script-run / app-build acceptance templates; task-type detection ignores file paths (`nubs-deploy-settings.yaml` is not a deploy).
- Deliverable capture: stdout of a SHELL transcript, never the command line; loopback URLs redacted at the relay funnel whatever authored the body.

## Cancel semantics
A user stop used to hard-stop one session while the wave launched the remaining lanes and verification kept lapping under the aborted turn's signal. Now: `interruptTask()` on the durable task; the lane plan honors the turn abort; task-owned verification runs detached from the user turn; a stop in the spawn window stops the newborn session (`interruptInFlightTasksForRoom` when no session exists yet); the cancelled launch is silent.

## Planning / routing
- A single page/app/script build is ONE lane (`collapsePlannerLanes`): "build me a lil tetris page" had been phased into four sequential sub-agents in the same dir.
- One create per origin message (claim map), redirect successors claim+gate, same-origin sends skip the redundant queue; `send` effect receipts settle on the redirect's durable task id or the prompt's stop reason (every send used to settle `AUTHORITATIVE_RECEIPT_MISSING`).
- SHELL fail-fast on unbounded `find`; `CODING_TOOLS_MAX_READ_LINES` default with `0` sentinel.
- A planner-invented `workdir` that happens to exist is ignored unless the request, a route/apps tree, or a known lane grounds it (the planner filled `workdir` on every create); the schema contract now says to omit it for new builds.

## Voice / relay
LLM-phrased coding-lane voice (`src/voice/phrase-for-user.ts` as the single prompt home: spawn acks, lifecycle lines, park notices); verify-churn suppression no longer eats the verdict-released relay; observed completion bodies relay verbatim; park notices never say "the work may still be fine" on a fabricated input.

## Removal
`opencode` adapter fully removed (submodule, vendor, bin, config, cockpit surface) — eliza-code is the native adapter.

## Live acceptance (2026-08-22, Discord, gemma-4-31b)
fresh script 21s lap-1 · rerun 17s lap-1 · page build 26s with link · follow-up edit 30s · mid-build status 3s · cancel at +6s → task `interrupted`, zero sessions, one confirmation · impossible-input script → "the input was fabricated because the actual file is missing here, so the results aren't real" · plain chat 1s no spawn.

Pi was not runtime-tested because no Pi CLI was installed.1e:T1952,## Summary

- replace manual endpoint, pairing-code, and diagnostics setup with secure automatic native enrollment for Chrome, Firefox, and Safari
- add the desktop enrollment broker, native host lifecycle, browser/profile binding, durable revocation, replay protection, and short-lived credentials
- minimize the normal product UI: remove app-side bridge installation/admin controls, hide empty session policy, and reduce the popup to status plus one browser-required permission or recovery action
- add exact-current-site permission handling, 30-second app readiness retry, authenticated reconnect, and browser-owned all-sites permission
- harden Chrome release identity, Safari App Group/signing/provisioning, Windows named-pipe ACL/DPAPI storage, cookie validation, and loopback-only broker routing

Closes the implementation scope tracked in #23898. Store publication and physical-platform acceptance remain authority/hardware gated as documented below.

## Architecture and security

- Chrome/Firefox use native messaging with exact extension identities; Safari uses its containing-app handler and registered App Group secret.
- Enrollment messages are framed, bounded, HMAC-authenticated, caller-bound, and replay-protected.
- Credentials are capped at five minutes; revocation is durable per owner/browser/profile and cannot be bypassed by reinstalling or locally resetting the extension.
- The desktop broker accepts loopback API bases only. There is no unauthenticated localhost auto-pairing path.
- Browser site access remains an explicit browser-controlled permission. Current-site mode requests only the active HTTP(S) origin; all-sites mode uses the browser-owned warning.

## Minimal automatic UI

- The Eliza Browser view no longer exposes build/install, reveal-folder, open-extension-manager, refresh, endpoint, or pairing controls.
- Empty session security UI hides; active approvals and intercepted account-affecting actions remain visible.
- The extension popup has no pairing JSON, endpoint, credential, or diagnostics form.
- Connected/retrying states need no action. Human actions appear only for browser permission, explicit disconnect/revocation recovery, or disconnecting an active companion.
- Remaining controls meet the repository 44 px target.

## Review repairs at exact head\n\n- Revoked browsers now expose one owner-authenticated reset only when needed; the extension observes the reset automatically on its bounded 30-second alarm without weakening the durable broker tombstone.\n- Browser-wide reinstall-resistant revocation reset is atomic across profiles and proven by real PGlite coverage.\n- Unsigned Firefox XPI output is submission-only and never advertised as permanently installable without an AMO URL/signature.\n- Support claims are narrowed to the implemented Chrome, Firefox, and macOS Safari targets; retired pairing-JSON guidance is removed.\n- The current-base duplicate Cloud test declarations that blocked hosted lint are removed; the full cloud-shared lint lane passes locally.\n\n## Publication and physical acceptance blockers

These require external authority or hardware and are not claimed by source tests:

- Chrome Web Store item/publisher ID, matching Package-tab public key, publisher MFA/upload authority, and public support/privacy URLs
- Apple Team ID, signing identity/private key, registered app/extension identifiers, registered `group.ai.elizaos.browserbridge` App Group, distribution profiles, App Store Connect authority, and signed installed Safari acceptance
- Firefox AMO publisher/submission authority if AMO distribution is required
- physical Windows and Linux packaged-installer acceptance for host registration/uninstall, restart/revocation/re-enrollment, real browser actions, multi-monitor/DPI, lock/sleep, background/PiP, multi-session, virtual cursors, and OS security boundaries

Release packaging fails closed when required store identity, signing, or provisioning inputs are absent.

## Scope — the TASKS action surface + satellites

- **Cancel that cancels**: `runCancel` interrupts the durable task before session teardown (single, all, and room-fallback paths); an unmatched `search` is a miss, not a room-wide stop; room-wide interrupts are owner-scoped.
- **Send vs create routing on the user's words**: a send into a finished lane is classified follow-up / related / fresh from the user's own text (planner paraphrases dropped the anaphors); a new deliverable never inherits the predecessor's title, acceptance criteria, or route workdir (a prime-script ask was graded against a .ts-count contract and parked, live); workdir routes match the genuine turn's words only.
- **Titles/labels/slugs**: uncapped canonical title/label storage (develop's `completeUserReferenceView`/`labelFrom`), planner-junk sanity guards, and served slugs that stop at the first clause boundary (`tip-calculator-page`, not `tip-calculator-page-with-input`).
- **Spawn honesty**: post-attach interrupt re-check before the first prompt (a cancel landing mid-spawn found no session to stop and the build ran anyway); per-origin spawn caps; admin-stop stamps land before stops so a kill never narrates as a task failure.

Base: `feat/coding-agent-split-5-router-relays`. Next: 6b-satellites (cockpit/UI and satellite plugins). The core runtime delta is an independent PR.

# Risks

Medium. This changes the provisioning deployment workflow and installs a new
systemd daemon, but both runtime/scheduler gates are uniquely forced to literal
`0`. Disabled composition reads only those gates and performs no DB, provider,
KMS, SSH, registry, or field-encryption credential read.

Activation is explicitly out of scope and blocked: before either gate can be
`1`, backup authority must leave the shared host's `deploy` sudo/docker trust
domain (dedicated identity or host), execute an immutable verified artifact,
and receive credentials through root/PID 1. The prerequisite is documented in
`agent-backup.md` and `.env.example`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds a dedicated manifest-v3 backup-catalog daemon with cadence bounded to at
  most 60 seconds, retry metrics, redacted health, and bounded shutdown.
- Composes the existing scheduler/catalog, authenticated Agent capture, KMS key
  bundles, affinity-aware durable spool, native R2 primary, Hetzner S3
  secondary, publication/verification, retention, deletion, and GC paths.
- Hardens runtime authority, cancellation, deadline, response-loss replay,
  partial-spool cleanup, exact provider deletion, and catalogue settlement.
- Adds a strict atomic systemd EnvironmentFile helper and fail-safe deployment:
  stop/disable old authority before safe-off replacement, install helper/unit
  through exact-SHA root-owned candidates, verify effective unit identity,
  fragment, drop-ins, and ExecStart before enabling.
- Keeps periodic admission and worker runtime disabled by default and forced off
  by deployment. It does not enroll the fleet, activate credentials, remove the
  legacy writer, mutate a live provider, or advertise an RPO.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature plus non-breaking safety fixes for the dormant Sandboxes V3 backup
worker path.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation is required and updated: the environment contract, bounds,
disabled deployment behavior, and activation trust-domain prerequisite are
documented.

## What changed

- adds strict `ContentReference`, `ReadSlice`, and `ReadView` contracts with exact half-open ranges, revision-bound continuation, completeness, and SHA-256 page proofs;
- adds opt-in planner/evaluator projection through `ELIZA_PROGRESSIVE_CONTENT_PROJECTION`, with bounded model views that always retain an addressable complete source;
- adds production paging for coding FILE, stored documents, stored attachment text, room-scoped memories, Gmail bodies, and persisted terminal-output attachments;
- pushes document range authorization into the database adapter and keeps authorization attached to every continuation read;
- rejects stale revisions, expired/revoked grants, malformed ranges, and incomplete search locators explicitly;
- adds deterministic corpus, benchmark, isolated scenario, evidence ingestion, implementation specification, research report, and staged rollout plan.

This does not make resource use unbounded. Transport admission, protocol frames, per-read response size, process capture, and provider request budgets remain explicit. The difference is that a bounded model/transport view now carries a verifiable continuation to complete durable content instead of silently destroying the tail.

# Risks

Large. This changes the primary settings experience in the cloud-only macOS desktop build and touches authentication, runtime targeting, connector mutations, voice, shortcuts, native storage, and window behavior. The standard settings experience remains mounted for non-cloud-only builds.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- adds the native-looking cloud-only desktop settings panel (elizaOS primitives; the earlier NuPhy dependency was replaced in-branch)
- aligns Discord, Telegram, WhatsApp, Twilio, Blooio, Google, and Microsoft forms and mutations with current Cloud API contracts
- requires an affirmative `success === true` from connector mutations — `{}` or a malformed 2xx surfaces a failure instead of fabricating a connected state
- makes destructive connector actions identity-explicit: Discord connect requires choosing the target agent; OAuth/Discord disconnect binds the exact connection id chosen in the dialog
- posts the real `POST /api/v1/mcps` contract (`description` required, `endpointType: "external"`, `externalEndpoint`)
- scopes Reset app state / Clear cache to elizaOS-owned storage keys and cache names; unrelated same-origin state survives (regression-tested)
- wires Sign out / Connect Cloud to the real `handleCloudSignOut` / `handleInteractiveCloudLogin` boundaries (the dead custom events are gone)
- refreshes connector rows immediately after successful connect and disconnect mutations
- fixes narrow navigation so the back button returns to the settings hub
- wires supported desktop controls to real native/backend operations
- keeps direct Cloud login, production personal-agent binding, runtime-mode persistence, packaged native keyring loading, and desktop transport behavior
- persists push-to-talk shortcuts and restores the prior shortcut when a replacement is rejected
- annotates every retained catch in the panel with the repository `error-policy:J<N>` categories

This PR intentionally lands the hardened panel first. #24495 time-boxes the follow-up migration into the canonical `SettingsView` and deletion of the parallel cloud-panel tree within one release.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature and non-breaking bug fixes for the cloud-only desktop build.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user documentation change is included. The architecture follow-up and deletion criteria are recorded in #24495.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start at `packages/ui/src/components/settings/cloud-panel/CloudSettingsPanel.tsx`, then the contract helpers in `cloud-connector-contracts.ts` (`connectorMutationSucceeded`, `buildMcpCreatePayload`), `ConnectionsSection.tsx` (identity-explicit connect/disconnect), `AdvancedSection.tsx` (scoped reset + real sign-out), and the Electrobun runtime-mode tests.

## Detailed testing steps

1. Build and install this exact cloud-only macOS head.
2. Clear the prior desktop session and launch the installed application.
3. Sign in against `https://eliza.app` and verify the production personal-agent target is selected.
4. Open settings at wide and sub-700px widths. Verify the narrow back action returns to the hub.
5. Exercise each relevant General, Voice, Agent, Connections, Permissions, Notifications, Shortcuts, and Advanced control.
6. Connect and disconnect a live connector. Verify the identity picker binds the exact record and the row updates without leaving the section.
7. Restart the app and verify persisted runtime, agent, and shortcut state.
8. Verify push-to-talk, STT, and TTS with captured audio.

23:T17e7,## Summary

This draft stacks the connector-owned portion of #24288 on #24363 without adding a second authority or dispatcher.

- adds an additive `MessageConnector.sendPreparedInteraction` seam whose input is only host-prepared, renderer-safe data
- declares exhaustive canonical profiles for every actual first-party production message connector
- implements host-prepared native adapters and authenticated inbound consumption for Slack, WhatsApp Cloud, Discord, and Telegram
- uses explicit semantic text/conversational fallback for Gmail, Google Chat, iMessage, Instagram, Matrix, WeChat, X, WhatsApp Baileys, and unsupported block/provider combinations
- strips raw markers and keeps authorization, bindings, effects, auth state, and secrets out of provider payloads
- adds an AST-derived `.ts`/`.tsx` production registration inventory that counts multiple registrations and account-provider extensions
- keeps Signal visible as an explicit non-registration (`SIGNAL_DIRECT_TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE`) rather than claiming a connector that does not exist

Refs #24288. Depends on #24287 / #24363.

## Intentional draft hold

This PR does **not** claim real round-trip acceptance or close #24288. The current exact foundation head remains held by its latest review:

1. oversized conversational payloads may still negotiate despite lossless delivery not being proven;
2. replay status is derived from a pre-consume snapshot rather than a discriminated atomic consume outcome;
3. PID-only stale-lock recovery is unsafe under PID reuse;
4. committed/completed sessions are never pruned, so the bounded store eventually reaches permanent capacity exhaustion.

Those are host/store authority defects and remain in #24363; this branch does not duplicate or rewrite that authority. Real provider/executor evidence also remains unavailable until credentials and supported live targets are supplied.

- standalone Discord/Telegram typechecks remain unavailable because the workspace cannot resolve pre-existing `@elizaos/vault` and `@elizaos/plugin-commands` declarations; both production builds pass.
- the core build generated Node/browser/edge/testing outputs successfully, but the build process retained an open handle after its success banner and required interruption; core typecheck passes.

## Outcome

- Replaces the task-coordinator plaintext API-key/autosave surface with the canonical Models & Providers + encrypted Accounts flow.
- Adds one validated atomic coding policy for backend, powerful/fast model, ordered fallbacks, approval preset, linked-account provider/IDs/strategy, and expected billing source.
- Gives the owner SETTINGS action the same full read/write contract for chat and voice.
- Revalidates policy at spawn, fails closed on unusable pinned accounts or exhausted fallback routes, and stamps non-secret provider/account/model/billing receipts.
- Exposes Codex, Claude, OpenCode, Kimi Code (user-attended OAuth), Grok Build (user-attended pinned OAuth), and elizaOS backends.
- Routes Z.AI Coding Plan accounts through OpenCode using the dedicated supported-tool coding endpoint and subscription-quota identity; it never aliases them to Z.AI general PAYG.
- Keeps Kimi/Moonshot API and xAI API billing separate from Kimi/Grok subscriptions; OpenRouter remains credits/BYOK rather than a subscription.
- Separates expected billing policy from observed credential source and leaves actual billing null unless a provider receipt verifies it.
- Preserves all OpenRouter model IDs within the 1 MiB response guard and keeps OpenCode model entry free-form.
- Keeps DeepSeek explicitly API/PAYG-only because no first-party DeepSeek coding subscription surface is available.

25:T11b2,## Relates to

Relates to #22903 and implements the maintainer-requested standalone carve-out from closed #23162.

## What this PR does

- adds a standalone three-attempt stability plan and aggregate report contract to `@elizaos/scenario-runner`;
- adds a programmatic executor over an injected current-runtime adapter, with unconditional attempts 1, 2, and 3 for every scenario/provider/model cell;
- requires strict `3/3`, records first-attempt success, returns a deterministic focus list below `3/3`, and retains evidence on budget failures;
- assigns unique attempt IDs and output paths, compares runtime-produced initial-state hashes, enforces timeout/token/tool budgets, always tears down with a separately abortable bounded deadline, and distinguishes scenario from harness failures;
- adds the `eliza-scenarios stability` plan/aggregate command; malformed or undeclared skipped reports are configuration failures (exit 2), while a valid below-`3/3` aggregate exits 1;
- binds artifact reads and exclusive writes to regular-file and ancestor-directory device/inode identities before and after access;
- exports the plan, aggregation, and executor APIs and documents the adapter's responsibility to create a fresh runtime/process, database, mock-service state, caches, and model conversation per attempt.

This diff is deliberately limited to nine `packages/scenario-runner` files. It does **not** add `packages/synthetic-world`, a mock-world server, model-fixture migration, live workflow, or any dependency on the closed infrastructure foundation.

# Risks

Medium. This adds a public connector capability resolver and durable authorization/dispatch boundary. A bad connector profile conservatively degrades presentation but cannot grant callback authority. The bundled JSON adapter is intentionally single-host; multi-host deployments must supply a transactional store and idempotent effect/outbox implementation.

# What this PR does

- Defines one versioned, immutable capability profile per concrete connector account/target and negotiates native, conversational, signed-hosted, or sensitive delivery from typed capability and payload size.
- Adds durable sessions binding actor, audience, agent, connector account, room, source message, response schema and byte limits, authorization revision, expiry, effect, lease state, replay key, and retained receipt.
- Uses opaque callback references containing no answers, authorization, credentials, trusted bindings, or effects.
- Adds the registered `MessageInteractionHost` authority. Render preparation returns only the renderer-safe block, negotiated delivery, callback, expiry, profile ID, and an already-validated signed-hosted URL when selected. Canonical consume accepts restart-safe connector-authenticated actor/audience/agent/account/room bindings plus the provider receipt, while the original source-message binding remains host-retained, claims by provider inbound event ID, invokes only a host-owned effect handler, and persists the app-state result and audit receipt.
- Enforces fail-closed expiry, revocation, cross-actor/room/account/agent denial, provider mismatch/collision denial, receipt validation, replay, and crash/lease recovery.
- Implements a hardened single-host file adapter with 0600 files, no-follow and directory identity checks, same-filesystem atomic replace/fsync, cross-process locking and liveness recovery, corruption failure, and retention GC.
- Commits a deterministic capability matrix covering all production `registerMessageConnector` roots; Signal remains an explicit unsupported exclusion.

# What this fixes

- Aborts an in-flight Android Cloud login when the user cancels instead of allowing a late poll, credential write, browser operation, or session/history restore to authenticate the shell.
- Makes acknowledgement-required credential delivery atomic: the exact session-bound key is deactivated before plaintext reveal and activated only in the same primary transaction as receipt acknowledgement.
- Gives new CLI keys an exact `eliza_cli_<64 hex>` identity, bypasses positive API-key/inference caches, and revalidates them from the primary so neither pre-ACK use nor a stale cache refill can authenticate.
- Persists a non-authenticating provisional credential before ACK. Response loss, revocation failure, and process restart retain exact cleanup ownership; simultaneous quarantine/revocation failure is neutralized locally when possible.
- Stores an enumerable multi-record credential journal so failed A cleanup cannot be overwritten by B, rejects malformed journals without altering secure-store bytes, and retains exact response-loss receipts until revocation is confirmed.
- Serializes attempt-owned secure credential mutations so stale A cannot clear, acknowledge, or revoke newer B, and exposes PATCH/DELETE through the thin CLI-session dispatcher without capturing authenticated `/complete`.
- Rejects HEAD before the single-use reveal path, serializes ACK and cleanup through session/key row locks, and retains authenticated revocation receipts until their bound key expires or is deleted.
- Uses attempt-scoped voice ownership, retries teardown after a late native start settles, retains failed listener/removal handles, and binds every generated recognizer callback to its exact instance and epoch so stale A cannot notify or stop B.
- Translates native Android recognition codes into actionable recovery guidance while preserving terminal teardown and visible listening-state reset.
- Adds deterministic regressions across secure-store failure/relaunch, response-loss ACK/revoke, concurrent cancel/ack, cache refill, shared-runtime/inference ingress, browser-close hangs, late voice start, recognizer error, and teardown failure.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts.
- [ ] `bun run verify` passes post-sync, or the exact unrelated blocker is documented in **Known gaps / failures** below.

# Risks

Medium. This adds a public scenario-runner mutation/reset API. Reset is destructive by design, so it requires a versioned receipt, verifies the runtime owner plus per-record namespace markers, deletes only the exact recorded identifiers through `AgentRuntime`, and proves absence. Unsupported domain categories fail closed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Validates a versioned synthetic manifest completely before writes, rejecting unsupported versions, unknown fields, duplicate IDs/pairs, bad references, wrong owners, and unsupported domain categories.
- Applies worlds, entities, rooms, participants, memories, relationships, and tasks through the real `IAgentRuntime` production CRUD boundaries. There is no parallel `WorldData` store.
- Uses deterministic namespace IDs where production contracts accept caller IDs. For SQL relationships, it deliberately ignores the non-authoritative batch return and records the persisted ID from `getRelationshipsByPairs()` readback.
- Returns a JSON-serializable receipt containing the exact created identifiers. Apply failure compensates and proves absence when possible; an ambiguous relationship outcome returns `SCENARIO_MANIFEST_DIRTY` with the receipt instead of claiming rollback.
- Projects a stable canonical snapshot from production stores, verifies receipt ownership before reset, supports idempotent/concurrent reset convergence, and proves byte-equivalent reset/reseed readback.
- Documents that schedules, notifications, approvals, and provider state remain unsupported until their existing production services/readback contracts are integrated.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature: a non-breaking scenario-runner persistence foundation.

# Documentation changes needed?

The scenario-runner README now documents the supported v1 boundary, receipt/reset contract, and explicit unsupported categories.

2e:T2025,## Summary

Progresses #24299 and replaces the audit-closed #24336 and #24480. This replacement adds the permission-checked opaque artifact resolver requested in the #24480 audit: a secure-root regression proves omitted bytes are retrievable through the actual SHELL action without exposing or authorizing the state-root path.

- add an explicit per-turn `codingMode` that enters the planner directly, uses a compact stable prompt and focused native tools, and avoids the ordinary chat response-handler/evaluator round trip
- propagate coding mode and truthful terminal failure through the code CLI, client, ACP adapter, and standalone benchmark path
- make standalone benchmarks await deferred coding-capability registration and fail before inference if READ/WRITE/EDIT/SHELL are absent
- bound file/shell-history context while preserving complete oversized shell stdout/stderr as retention-limited artifacts; the returned opaque handle is bound to the producing agent and conversation and pages redacted streams through the SHELL action
- harden READ/WRITE/EDIT against stale pagination, accidental overwrite, literal-escape corruption, secret-redaction false positives, and post-mutation inspection deduplication
- require successful post-mutation SHELL evidence; repeated-call forced synthesis now returns a typed failure instead of fabricated success
- add one canonical typed child terminal result across native orchestration and bridge surfaces, with state, evidence, blockers/questions, lineage, delivery, and verification
- harden credential redemption and verifier scheduling against authorization changes, infrastructure-inconclusive verdicts, and criteria-free validation deadlocks

This PR deliberately does **not** claim complete Pi parity. It fixes trace-proven runtime defects and materially closes the efficiency gap, while the controlled sample remains too small and Claude is still unauthenticated.

## Architecture findings

Pi's coding loop is intentionally small: a stable system prefix, a compact append-only conversation, four primary file/shell tools, bounded observations, and model-owned termination. Pi does not implement ACP in core; `pi-acp` adapts it. OpenClaw's current native runtime reaches Pi externally through `acpx -> pi-acp`.

Eliza can express the same loop as a plugin configuration, and this PR adds that direct lane. Eliza retains real advantages for long-running work: plugin composition, durable projects/tasks, services, independent evaluators, secure credential brokering, typed parent/child state, delivery tracking, and restart recovery. The prior inefficiency came from applying the generic chat pipeline to coding turns: extra selection/evaluation calls, broader prompt/action surfaces, unbounded observations, and ambiguous terminal egress.

## Controlled trace benchmark

The lightweight sample used two Flipt tasks from the checked-in SWE-bench Pro corpus (2/731, 0.27%). Full logical trajectories, patches, command logs, normalized usage, timings, graders, and SHA-256 manifests are retained locally under `/private/tmp/coding-parity-runs/final/`. The corrected hidden graders are pinned to each exact base; earlier fixture-inconsistent graders were excluded.

| Agent / lane | Task outcome | Calls | Tools / failures | Prompt or input | Cache ratio | Max context | Wall |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Pi 0.84.2 / Cerebras gpt-oss-120b | whitespace FAIL; comma regression, false success | 20 | 19 / 7 | normalized aggregate | 83.26% | 24,054 | 95.25s |
| Pi 0.84.2 / Cerebras gpt-oss-120b | env FAIL; syntax-invalid, unfinished | 6 | 6 / 1 unfinished | normalized aggregate | 63.92% | recorded | 46.03s |
| Eliza pre-fix / Cerebras gpt-oss-120b | whitespace FAIL; source corruption | 12 | 10 / 6 | 170,230 | 64.82% | 25,944 | 111.64s |
| Eliza after automatic revisions | whitespace FAIL; fabricated pseudo-tool success | 6 | 2 / 0 | 34,250 | 52.69% | 7,382 estimated | 13.96s |
| Eliza final controlled code | whitespace **hidden PASS**, typed terminal failure because no test command passed | 14 | 12 / 6 | 200,560 | 69.37% | 24,822 estimated | 123.94s |
| Eliza controlled env, before final loop fixes | env FAIL | 14 | 13 / 10 | 186,230 | 92.86% | 15,589 | 15.68s |
| Codex CLI 0.144.1 / gpt-5.6-sol | whitespace PASS | 1 turn | 17 | 841,645 | 91.46% | native aggregate | 327.24s |
| Codex CLI 0.144.1 / gpt-5.6-sol | env interrupted before terminal; compile FAIL | incomplete | 19 | unavailable | unavailable | unavailable | interrupted |
| Claude Code | not run: local CLI unauthenticated | — | — | — | — | — | — |

The final Eliza whitespace candidate is functionally correct under the hidden grader and terminally conservative under its own evidence: broad/local test attempts failed because the harness had no persistent Go module-cache environment and unrelated broad tests required unavailable services. The trace directly motivated documenting that every SHELL invocation is a fresh process. No successful agent-visible test means the runtime correctly refuses to claim verified success.

The strongest measured efficiency improvement is causal: automatic session revision tracking reduced the same controlled Eliza task from 85 to 6 calls, 14 to 0 tool failures, and 1.49M to 34k prompt tokens before the next terminal bug became visible. The subsequent runtime fix prevents READ-after-EDIT suppression and converts unverified forced synthesis into typed failure.

This PR deliberately does **not** claim complete Pi parity. It fixes trace-proven runtime defects and materially closes the efficiency gap, while the controlled sample remains too small and Claude is still unauthenticated.

## Architecture findings

Pi's coding loop is intentionally small: a stable system prefix, a compact append-only conversation, four primary file/shell tools, bounded observations, and model-owned termination. Pi does not implement ACP in core; `pi-acp` adapts it. OpenClaw's current native runtime reaches Pi externally through `acpx -> pi-acp`.

Eliza can express the same loop as a plugin configuration, and this PR adds that direct lane. Eliza retains real advantages for long-running work: plugin composition, durable projects/tasks, services, independent evaluators, secure credential brokering, typed parent/child state, delivery tracking, and restart recovery. The prior inefficiency came from applying the generic chat pipeline to coding turns: extra selection/evaluation calls, broader prompt/action surfaces, unbounded observations, and ambiguous terminal egress.

## Controlled trace benchmark

The lightweight sample used two Flipt tasks from the checked-in SWE-bench Pro corpus (2/731, 0.27%). Full logical trajectories, patches, command logs, normalized usage, timings, graders, and SHA-256 manifests are retained locally under `/private/tmp/coding-parity-runs/final/`. The corrected hidden graders are pinned to each exact base; earlier fixture-inconsistent graders were excluded.

| Agent / lane | Task outcome | Calls | Tools / failures | Prompt or input | Cache ratio | Max context | Wall |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Pi 0.84.2 / Cerebras gpt-oss-120b | whitespace FAIL; comma regression, false success | 20 | 19 / 7 | normalized aggregate | 83.26% | 24,054 | 95.25s |
| Pi 0.84.2 / Cerebras gpt-oss-120b | env FAIL; syntax-invalid, unfinished | 6 | 6 / 1 unfinished | normalized aggregate | 63.92% | recorded | 46.03s |
| Eliza pre-fix / Cerebras gpt-oss-120b | whitespace FAIL; source corruption | 12 | 10 / 6 | 170,230 | 64.82% | 25,944 | 111.64s |
| Eliza after automatic revisions | whitespace FAIL; fabricated pseudo-tool success | 6 | 2 / 0 | 34,250 | 52.69% | 7,382 estimated | 13.96s |
| Eliza final controlled code | whitespace **hidden PASS**, typed terminal failure because no test command passed | 14 | 12 / 6 | 200,560 | 69.37% | 24,822 estimated | 123.94s |
| Eliza controlled env, before final loop fixes | env FAIL | 14 | 13 / 10 | 186,230 | 92.86% | 15,589 | 15.68s |
| Codex CLI 0.144.1 / gpt-5.6-sol | whitespace PASS | 1 turn | 17 | 841,645 | 91.46% | native aggregate | 327.24s |
| Codex CLI 0.144.1 / gpt-5.6-sol | env interrupted before terminal; compile FAIL | incomplete | 19 | unavailable | unavailable | unavailable | interrupted |
| Claude Code | not run: local CLI unauthenticated | — | — | — | — | — | — |

The final Eliza whitespace candidate is functionally correct under the hidden grader and terminally conservative under its own evidence: broad/local test attempts failed because the harness had no persistent Go module-cache environment and unrelated broad tests required unavailable services. The trace directly motivated documenting that every SHELL invocation is a fresh process. No successful agent-visible test means the runtime correctly refuses to claim verified success.

The strongest measured efficiency improvement is causal: automatic session revision tracking reduced the same controlled Eliza task from 85 to 6 calls, 14 to 0 tool failures, and 1.49M to 34k prompt tokens before the next terminal bug became visible. The subsequent runtime fix prevents READ-after-EDIT suppression and converts unverified forced synthesis into typed failure.

## Content-budget closure response (supersedes #24419)

Every cited cap is converted:
- **Criteria count/char limits**: gone — the complete refined criteria set is the contract (no 5-item slice, no 240-char cut); filter-dropped criteria persist as `droppedCriteria` task metadata for audit.
- **Captured tool output sliced**: evidence tool-output sections persist the COMPLETE text via the new content-addressed store and project reference-bearing views.
- **Orchestrator references sliced / workdir traversal caps**: fs-verified file contents project with per-file references; workdir enumeration carries explicit continuation notes naming what was not traversed.
- **Foundation**: `durable-content-store.ts` — sha256-addressed records under the trajectory dir; `durableProjection()` persists FIRST and the marker names the real resolver (`GET /api/orchestrator/content/:sha256?offset=&limit=`, in part 3). A textual marker alone is never emitted for dropped bytes.

## Scope — orchestrator foundation modules (leaves only; no service/action integrators)

**The durable-content contract this series is built on** — `services/durable-content.ts`:
- `boundedContentView(full, budget, ref)`: every capped string handed to a model prompt or chat surface is a VIEW; a partial view ends with a continuation marker naming its reference and carries a core `ReadView` (`ContentReference`/`ReadSlice` from #24305) with sha256'd slice/source.
- `orchestratorContentRef(scope, id)`: opaque `acpx-*` tokens; resolvers are the orchestrator's own HTTP surfaces (`/api/coding-agents/:id/output`, `/api/orchestrator/tasks/:id`).
- `canonicalProviderPair`: the canonical/provider convention from the #24262 repair (`canonicalPrTitle`/`providerPrTitle`) as a reusable helper — canonical persisted in full, provider payload derived explicitly and auditable.
- `subagent-stdout-log.ts` gains `readSubagentStdout(sessionId, {offset, limit})` — the chunk-indexed continuation reader over the durable per-session transcript.

**Verification foundations**: fabricated-input detection with pre-run existence/SHA-256 `InputBaseline`s (a write is fabrication evidence only when the input was captured absent before execution) + read/write verb-window target selection; acceptance criteria, completion evidence (full FS-verified file contents for content criteria), residuals, ground-truth/independent/goal verifiers.

**Routing foundations**: `ask-shapes.ts` (follow-up vs new-deliverable vs anaphor shapes), `follow-up-origin.ts` (a delivered follow-up re-keys the session's voice so its completion posts — see part 5), workdir route grounding, framework/account/model-gateway selection (OpenCode-free, aligned with develop's removal).

All modules are leaves: nothing here imports the ACP service, task service, router, or actions. Tests included per module.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds an instance-owned production `RegistryClient` and resettable real-HTTP registry/npm upstream.
- Bounds registry admission before schema parsing: JSON content type, identity encoding, 16 MiB body, fatal UTF-8, depth 32, width 10,000, nodes 100,000, and string size 256 KiB, with stable typed failures and stream cancellation.
- Preserves disk cache age, bounds disk reads and headers, caches upstream authority only, reapplies local overlays on every read, returns mutation-isolated clones, and generation-fences promotion/temp writes.
- Defines caller-isolated cancellation over shared singleflight: one caller can abort without cancelling the authority load or another caller.
- Drives the real installer/package manager against loopback artifacts. Bun partial/timeout output is removed before npm fallback; inability to prove a clean target fails closed before npm or Git fallback.
- Covers generated/index fallback, ETag/304, virtual TTL, reset/replay, malformed/adversarial responses, cancellation, real tar bytes, SRI provenance, missing artifacts, and integrity mismatch.

The branch intentionally contains a visibly attributed replay of open PR #24118 as a prerequisite; it does not hide or duplicate ownership of that fix.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature/fix: non-breaking deterministic test infrastructure plus production boundary hardening.

## What changed since #24549

The closing review's ruling — an automatic bounded projection of model-facing content violates the prompt-integrity contract even when a durable reference makes the omitted bytes recoverable later — is now the foundation's design:

- **No automatic projections anywhere.** `durableProjection`/`boundedContentView` (head + marker substitution) are deleted. Every path that reaches a model call, prompt, provider, action result, judge/verifier input, relay body, or prompt-rendered metadata carries the COMPLETE canonical text (well-formed Unicode + credential redaction — security transforms, not caps).
- **Durable store = durability + caller-requested retrieval only.** `persistDurableContent` content-addresses the complete canonical text; `GET /api/orchestrator/content/:sha256?offset=&limit=` serves caller-requested, code-point-snapped windows with an explicit continuation contract (actual byte range served, `hasMore`, `nextOffset`). References ride as metadata NEXT TO complete content, never instead of it.
- **Real hard boundaries fail typed.** Where an external protocol limit genuinely exists, dispatch is rejected with a typed error before any partial payload; internal storage is never treated as a boundary.

## Scope

Foundation modules only: the durable content store + retrieval route, routing shapes, verification helpers. Standalone and develop-targeted — it merges on its own.

Per the closing guidance on #24553/#24424, the remaining parts will be submitted one at a time against current develop as their predecessor merges (never as a stacked chain): ACP service → task service → router relays → actions surface → satellites. Each is already rebuilt on this lossless foundation and green locally (full plugin suite green: 3,344 passed).

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds a consumer-first observation boundary that wraps existing connector,
repository, service, or use-case entry points. It records sanitized canonical
request/response/readback hashes, generation, idempotency identity, worker/task
retry lineage, typed acceptance, injected-clock timestamps, and failure detail.

`succeeded` is impossible unless the real boundary call ran, returned an
accepted result, authoritative readback exists and matches, and the active
generation is still current. Timeout, retryable/permanent, rate-limit, partial,
ambiguous, and stale-completion scripts all fail without fabricated success.

The integration proof wraps the production scheduling
`dispatchViaMessageConnector` path and classifies its typed `DispatchResult`.
Restart proof reopens the same append-only ledger, preserves the prior
generation, rejects a stale completion, and continues ordering in the new one.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature (non-breaking scenario/evidence infrastructure).

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated `packages/scenario-runner/README.md` with the authority boundary,
success/readback rule, fault contract, and #24076 single-writer prerequisite.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

adapter; the remaining cases prove the negative and restart contracts.

# Risks

Medium. This adds an authenticated subprocess control boundary used by scenario and Cloud test infrastructure. Strict decoding, generation fencing, dirty-session diagnostics, atomic teardown acknowledgements, timeout bounds, and fully redacted malformed-response failures constrain the risk. # Background

## What does this PR do?

- Publishes one versioned `@elizaos/shared/synthetic-control` command/result protocol, codec, authenticated client, transport handler, and reset-bound session lifecycle.
- Mounts that handler on the real Cloud control-plane mock process only when an explicit authority and token are injected.
- Gives scenario-runner and Cloud E2E consumers the same explicit client/session construction path.
- Exercises health, lease acquire/release, seed, reset, time advance, fault install/clear, snapshot, ledger query, and teardown across an independent Bun OS process.
- Makes invalid input, auth failure, authority failure, timeouts, concurrent/reset races, partial seed mutation, crash/restart, stale generations, and teardown acknowledgement deterministic and observable without fabricated success. Malformed provider replies are reduced to a fixed public error with no raw parser cause, and regression coverage checks message, stack, string, JSON, recursive-cause, and runtime-inspection representations for distinctive response secrets.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature: shared synthetic-world subprocess test protocol.

# Risks

Medium. This replaces test-maintained interaction subsets with production AST/registration discovery. Static analysis can under- or over-classify dynamic handlers, so unknown declarations and unproven activation paths fail closed instead of synthesizing parity.

# What this PR does

Generates deterministic ignored JSON and Markdown operation ledgers on demand from exact production declarations. Current discovery independently reconciles 39 runtime view declarations plus 11 named shell/overlay registrations, minus seven overlaps, into 43 unique surfaces. It removes 2,626 lines of hand-maintained view-owner/minimum-count ratchets and records stable operation IDs, owner/use case, typed IO/errors, auth/capability gates, idempotency/confirmation, delivery evidence, bounded view-only reasons, sensitive boundaries, and widget/chat/voice channels.

Handler analysis follows local declarations and known mutation primitives, including non-GET `fetch`, mutation/service/API calls, and common semantic verbs. Agent metadata is not accepted as runtime proof: clickable agent controls require source-backed handler evidence, and declaration-only action/capability names remain unverified until linked to a concrete contract.

Current production result at `390e5cde1f`: 1,974 operations across 43 surfaces; 420 semantic mutations (163 proven agent-delivered, eight secure exceptions, 155 view-only violations). The unresolved inventory contains 290 entries across 180 unique source sites: 155 business mutations, 41 missing-handler-evidence controls, and 94 unverified declaration contracts. This draft intentionally does not close #24280.

3b:T934,Part 5 of the split series closing out #24262 — the sub-agent router and completion relays.

cc @lalalune

## Content-budget closure response (supersedes #24422)

Rebased on the repaired parents: oversized deliverables project with resolvable references instead of vanishing (undefined-fallback removed where the user asked for the output); origin best-results persist as `canonicalBestResult` task metadata (the volatile LRU is no longer the only record); character bio/traits and fact values feed phrasing models COMPLETE (the cited "character text is sliced"); narration/notification bounds are named previews or reference-bearing views.

## Scope — relays that are honest, lane-scoped, and reference-bearing

- **Per-lane deferred relays**: completion relays defer until the verification verdict per `${taskId}\0${sessionId}`; a verdict releases only its own lane's relay; the re-engage path drops the lane's relay on every exit (the 5-minute fallback was releasing judged-failed completions as "passed"); a user-stopped task's relay is dropped regardless of deferral config.
- **Follow-up voice re-keying**: a follow-up delivered into a live session re-keys that session's request voice (`followUpOriginMessageId`, noted at queue time, activated at delivery) so its completion posts instead of dying as a duplicate terminal — "also give it a dark mode toggle" completed silently before (live).
- **Reference-bearing views**: the mid-run narration flush (800-char chat view) and an oversized asked-output deliverable name their continuation reference (`acpx-session-output:*`) instead of a bare truncation marker; the full content is durable on the session transcript. `recordOriginResult` keeps full text.
- **Diagnosable drops**: every completion-relay suppression decision (duplicate lineage, duplicate request terminal, verify churn, retry handoff) and each actual injection logs at warn — a dropped relay was previously undiagnosable from logs.
- Evaluators: completion/failure relays, durable-cancel routing, finished-work follow-up routing (a "run it again" follow-up routes to the finished lane; a new-deliverable ask that merely says "and run it" does not), route-scoped work routing.

Base: `feat/coding-agent-split-4-task-service`. Next: 6-actions-surface.

# Risks

Medium, fail-closed release-workflow change.

- The staging bearer is exposed only to the Chromium step. The first deployed page load is public; the exact top-level Pages origin, renderer manifest, and build-time staging API origin must all validate before browser storage receives the bearer. All three are revalidated after reload and in the fresh context.
- Wrangler Pages output is accepted only as the two audited `4.100.0` NDJSON records. Provider schema drift fails the release rather than manufacturing authority.
- Raw Wrangler output, Playwright traces/screenshots/video, browser proof inputs, bearer values, responses, and model content are never uploaded. Only closed authority and the final secret-free receipt are artifacts.
- The browser runs on `https://develop.eliza-app.pages.dev`. This proves the deployed Pages renderer on the canonical branch alias; it does not exercise hostname-gated behavior unique to `https://cloud-staging.eliza.app` and is not presented as #18045 warming proof.
- The real remote lane spends staging model credits only after merge and an explicit `run_deployed_renderer_staging=true` dispatch (or the existing repo/org opt-in). This PR does not arm a schedule or a global variable.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Binds the Pages deployment to the exact source SHA with `--commit-hash` and closes Wrangler's append-only output into a strict, privacy-safe authority record.
- Byte-verifies the canonical develop Pages alias in addition to staging custom domains.
- Adds a staging-only Chromium job after Pages deploy and routing verification. It accepts the existing repo/org opt-in or a one-shot dispatch input; the one-shot is restricted to `develop` + staging + `force=false` and fails explicitly when a fresh Pages deployment or routing proof is absent.
- Combines deployment authority, preflight, remote browser smoke, latency, continuity, and postflight into one strict proof bound to the same SHA, workflow run/attempt, renderer build, API origin, and latency.
- Keeps the existing schema-v2 local receipt honest. Schema v3 and `deployedRendererTested=true` can be derived only from the validated proof file; there is no boolean escape hatch.

Prior authors inventoried before the added caller/admission work. Existing proof surfaces: Shaw (`shawmakesmagic@gmail.com`, `shawgotbags@gmail.com`), nubs / NubsCarson (`nubs@nubs.site`), Stan / standujar (`s.andujar@proton.me`), and raynord22. Newly touched canonical deploy caller: Bill Ding, Claude Fable 5, Marco Guaspari Worms / MarcoWorms, Odilitime, Sayo, Shaw (`shaw@elizalabs.ai` plus the identities above), Sol, agent-cortex, dependabot[bot], lalalune (`elizamakesmagic@gmail.com`, `shaw.nicola.walters@gmail.com`), and the already listed Nubs/Stan/raynord22 identities. The new deployed-browser workflow contract had no base history.

Improvement: release attestation and deployed E2E coverage. No production UI implementation is changed.

3f:T1e14,# Relates to

Relates to #23099. This is a dormant, additive identity-authority foundation. It does not newly register or activate claim lifecycle consumers, ingest or backfill claims, expose claim mutation routes/actions, or cut over legacy reads. The SQL identity service already exists on `develop`; the new observation, verification, dispute, and revoke methods all fail closed until authenticated producers are implemented.

# Security outcome

- Observation cannot reserve a subject and fails with `IDENTITY_CLAIM_OBSERVATION_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` until a capability-bound, tenant/account/subject-scoped connector producer exists.
- Verify fails with `IDENTITY_VERIFICATION_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`; dispute/revoke fail with `IDENTITY_ADMIN_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`. Caller-supplied authority objects are rejected, and transition timestamps/cursors are validated before database access.
- Historical `verified` and `owner_bound` rows are not delivery or owner authorization. Both paths fail closed until independently authenticated producers prove the connector assertion and principal-to-owner relationship.
- No action, route, provider, backfill, or consumer cutover activates these dormant mutation contracts.

# Persistence and deletion

- Claim-journal `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, and `TRUNCATE` are denied to ordinary application roles with SQLSTATE `55000`; `TRUNCATE agents CASCADE` is also denied.
- Row-level parent-agent deletion remains the only supported lifecycle cascade. The real PostgreSQL lane proves commit removes the journal and rollback restores it.
- No retention table, deletion-evidence function, grant, schema/export, or application-visible deletion artifact is introduced. The real PostgreSQL lane checks catalogs and role grants for their absence.
- Deletion evidence is unavailable pending the #23098 out-of-transaction lifecycle saga and separately governed correlation-secret lifecycle. This PR makes no erasure-proof claim: same-transaction evidence would remain correlatable through PostgreSQL transaction metadata, WAL, replicas, backups, and query logs.
- Guards discover the visible deployment schema through PostgreSQL catalogs and use schema-qualified functions/tables; there is no `public` assumption in the implementation.
- Migration dry-run returns before post-migration guard, message-search, and RLS DDL.

# Migration inventory

The deterministic read-only inventory reports canonical claims; wrong-instance, unverified, disconnected, provider/external-subject/owner-identity mismatches; missing and foreign-tenant legacy entity identities and relationships; LifeOps UUID/existence/tenant/provider/live-account failures, including foreign rows that reference target-tenant principals/accounts; and account-scoped duplicate subjects. It performs no writes or identifier coercion.

# Prior-review closure

- Unsafe verification and administration authority: mutation methods fail closed; no caller JSON or historical row can authorize delivery/ownership.
- Journal immutability and lifecycle boundary: PGlite and PostgreSQL prove direct mutation/truncate denial, parent cascade, and rollback.
- Incomplete inventory: canonical, owner-binding, connector-account, runtime entity/relationship, LifeOps, metadata, and unscoped trust sources are inventoried deterministically.
- Compatibility: claim version/authority additions remain optional in contract v1 and new service methods have fail-closed defaults for existing subclasses.
- Retention defect: the proposed in-transaction ledger/function/grants/tests/docs were removed completely; only generic absence assertions remain.
- Input hardening: blank keys/reasons, non-finite limits, malformed cursors, and non-canonical RFC3339 instants are rejected.
