# Risks

Medium. This activates the existing SQL identity authority in default, Node, and browser plugin entries. The cutover is intentional: identity reads now fail closed through the canonical service instead of falling back to legacy metadata. This repository has no current users to migrate. The new attestation surface records evidence only; it cannot create redirects, merge journals, or delete principals.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds an immutable `identity_person_link_attestations` authority table with tenant, actor, role, transport, digest, idempotency, and generation evidence.
- Adds generation-fenced `attestPersonLink` and `verifyPersonLink` operations to the canonical `IdentityResolutionService` and SQL implementation.
- Registers `SqlIdentityResolutionService` in all SQL plugin runtimes.
- Adds private `POST /api/identity/person-links/attest` and `GET /api/identity/person-links/verify` routes.
- Derives actor principal and OWNER/ADMIN role from the authenticated server boundary. Request bodies cannot supply actor, role, authority, `confirmed`, or `verified` values.
- Installs a PostgreSQL/PGlite trigger that rejects attestation UPDATE and DELETE with SQLSTATE `55000`.
- Keeps principals intact and creates no canonical redirect or merge-journal row.

## What kind of change is this?

Security feature and intentional identity-authority hard cut.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated the SQL README and paired CLAUDE/AGENTS package guides.

71:T7b7,Part 3 of the split series closing out #24262.

cc @lalalune

## Content-budget closure response (supersedes #24420)

The cited 12,000-char ACP terminal-envelope truncation now persists the COMPLETE output through the content-addressed store and emits a reference-bearing projection — the marker names `GET /api/orchestrator/content/:sha256` (route in this part), so the omitted output is recoverable, not just labeled. Buffer evictions carry explicit counts plus the durable stream target; stderr overflows persist before slicing; stdout-log rotation keeps epoch generations instead of overwriting the ground truth; the native terminal 512KB bound stays a documented fail-closed typed rejection.

## Scope — the ACP subprocess service + session output surface

- **Whole-turn prompt watchdog**: the JSON-RPC request timer bounds only the `session/prompt` exchange; every await around it (reconnect attach, lease mint, workspace build, store writes) was unbounded — one hang held the session busy forever (live: a queued follow-up wedged its session 12+ minutes with no child process and no error). `sendPrompt` now races the whole turn at inner-timeout+60s and releases the busy flag with a structured `ACP_PROMPT_TURN_WEDGED` errored turn.
- **Durable transcript fallback**: `getSessionOutput` for a closed session reads the durable per-session NDJSON transcript instead of returning "" (the in-memory buffer is deleted on close; the content is not gone).
- **Output continuations**: `GET /api/coding-agents/:id/output?offset=<chunk>&lines=<n>` windows the durable log — the resolver for `acpx-session-output:*` content references minted in part 2.
- Session lifecycle/auth/credential handling as carried from the #24262 repaired head (warm spawn claim, model leases, no-Landlock fail-closed Codex modes).

## Safety boundary

This PR does **not** enable account deletion, subscription cancellation, provider revocation, backup erasure, or Shared-agent fallback. POST remains fail closed. No response fabricates a scheduled deletion receipt.

## Independent-review repairs

- `findOpenByUserAndOrganizationId` scopes receipt reads to both tenant identifiers and reads from primary for lifecycle decisions.
- Current membership state is validated before receipt lookup; missing, inactive, deleted, and anonymous current users cannot receive an old receipt through GET or POST.
- Idempotent insert conflict recovery is organization-bound, so a stale cross-organization row cannot be returned after a uniqueness conflict.
- The legacy unscoped service receipt helper was removed.
- GET translates typed account-state conflicts to HTTP 409 instead of a generic error.
- The browser validates untrusted status/receipt DTOs and renders distinct loading, error, transfer, unavailable, and existing-request states.
- The English `cloud.privacy.s7Body`, `s8Body`, and `s12Body` catalog entries contain the rendered fail-closed/support guidance; a real-provider component test verifies it.

Exact head: f3dbae8fbbc28d6e58f7e4c81ae9dbee3c7f9dd174:T227d,# Relates to

Grok-parity inline secrets/connector UX. Originally a three-PR stack (#24631, #24632 — closed and collapsed here). No pre-existing issue; plan discussed and executed in-session.

# What this does

Brings the Grok Bot connect-a-service chat experience to the Eliza chat panel, in three commits that each stand alone:

**1. Sensitive-request card states its security contract** (`bb20824`)
- Card chrome for `SensitiveRequestBlock`; header explainer "Masked input. It never lands in the transcript." on storable secret forms.
- Shield note under submit distinguishing the two delivery paths: "Stored encrypted in the agent's secret store — never posted to chat." vs the one-shot tunnel "Sent once to the waiting session — never stored, never posted to chat."
- Default submit label "Save securely"; saved status gets a green check. OAuth panel gains the same shield treatment. Presentation-only — the save/tunnel transports are unchanged.

**2. `[CONNECTOR:<pluginId>]` inline connector card** (`0fb9dd8`)
- New `ConnectorCardWidget`: brand icon + name + one-line description + a single context-aware CTA.
  - **Authorize** for OAuth-capable connectors — drives the existing `POST /api/connectors/:provider/oauth/start`, opens the consent URL (https-only, fail-closed), polls plugin state for the connected flip.
  - **Add token** for token connectors — masked inline form saving via `PUT /api/secrets` + plugin enable; the value never enters the transcript.
  - Passive **Connected** state when already enabled + configured.
- Registered as an inline-widget built-in with every gate updated: WebKit interactive-widget a11y guard, exact-set registry tests, `WIDGET_MATRIX.md`.
- `packages/core`: `stripDashboardOnlyMarkers` strips `[CONNECTOR:…]` so Telegram/Discord/API replies never leak the raw marker.
- `uiWidgets` model guide teaches the marker inside its 60-line/1200-token budget test.

**3. Dismissable choice prompts + flow choreography** (`1060611`)
- `ChoiceWidget` header ✕: local dismiss — nothing is sent to the agent, options lock, shell collapses to "Dismissed". First-run prompts stay non-dismissable (composer frozen behind them).
- Guide scripts the connect flow: confirm via `[CHOICE:connector-add]` (Add-it / Not-now) → on accept emit `[CONNECTOR:x]` + one-line close; never ask for tokens or paste auth links in chat text.

## Fix

- classify time-sensitive market, weather, news, sports, mutable-fact, and explicit-verification requests
- preflight a server-owned live public lookup and retain provider, checked time, and supporting source URLs
- require current-data replies to select an exact URL from the live receipt
- reject novel numbers, currency, URLs, and named attribution not present in the receipt
- fail closed when live verification is unavailable and recover usefully after correction
- buffer Telegram streaming until validation, then render a concise source/provider/timestamp line
- prevent duplicate external search calls while retaining one auditable action receipt

77:Tdf1,Closes #24341

## Decision

This issue is relevant, on mission, necessary, and fixes a real fail-open authentication-discovery boundary. The managed login proxy previously validated only the selected object and `oauth`, so missing required fields and wrong scalar types could be patched, cached, and returned as healthy provider availability.

## What changed

- validates the complete current Steward SDK discovery contract before patching or caching;
- requires passkey, email, SIWE, SIWS, Google, Discord, GitHub, Twitter, and OAuth with their declared types;
- validates optional provider booleans, OIDC/disabled arrays, and captcha types/enums;
- preserves unknown envelope, provider, captcha, and forward-compatible flat `data` fields;
- preserves current flat and legacy `{ ok, data }` response shapes;
- returns explicit `502 steward_upstream_invalid_response` with `Cache-Control: no-store` for malformed successful upstream responses;
- prevents malformed responses from entering the isolate/browser cache;
- makes HEAD discovery validate the same upstream GET representation, strip the downstream body, and prime only a validated cache entry.

78:T16ea,# Relates to

Refs #24092 (cloud transport), de-duplicates #24185 (shared-utils helpers only)

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and was rebased onto current `origin/develop` (`0076c563ff`) before publication.
- [x] The production gateway-webhook and message-router provider transports are exercised without replacing client/request methods.
- [x] This is a narrow cloud deadline slice; `#24185` shared-utils helpers and Telegram #23963 remain separate.

# Risks

Moderate. The repair preserves URL construction, auth, request bodies, and delivery semantics while adding owned timer/listener cleanup and a 64 KiB response ceiling. It changes timeout identity to `TimeoutError` and rejects oversized or hung provider bodies before success is recorded. Blooio typing indicator remains best-effort (J4) and does not fail delivery.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Bounds the live webhook delivery paths that remain bare `fetch` after `#24185`: `gateway-webhook` `Twilio` `sendReply`, `Blooio` `sendBlooioMessage` + `sendTypingIndicator`, and `shared` `message-router` `sendViaTwilio`. Each owns a clearable 30s operation deadline that races signal-ignoring transports, composes caller cancellation, and covers a capped 64 KiB response-body read before callers parse or persist success.

Helper-only scope for #24185 is preserved — this PR covers exactly the production gap flagged in `standujar`'s review of #24185 at `3e43b825`: `message-router` `sendViaTwilio` and gateway-webhook adapters still pin workers on a hung provider. The previous combined dup branch `6bd57676` is deleted.

## Remaining scope

- `shared-utils` `twitter/cloudflare/twilio/blooio-api.ts` deadlines remain in `#24185` (with its `cloud-bound-helpers.test.ts`).
- `Telegram` remains owned by `#23963` (stronger bounded transport — not touched here).
- `message-router` `sendViaBlooio` is already bounded via `blooioApiRequest` helper when `#24185` lands; not re-bounded here.

79:Te13,Closes the local-connector slice of #24247.

## Outcome

- keeps pinned `@whiskeysockets/baileys@7.0.0-rc14` as the single bundled, in-process local/personal WhatsApp transport; users do not install or run a bridge, daemon, CLI, or companion application
- retains authenticated frontend QR pair/status/stop/disconnect and native message-connector capabilities
- preserves the separate hosted Meta WhatsApp Business connector and its credential/redaction inventory; this branch does not modify `packages/cloud`
- remains compatible with Blooio as the shared public-number path; Blooio is not required for QR-linked personal WhatsApp
- removes duplicate Meta Graph/webhook authority from the local plugin so the local and hosted transports do not compete
- stages remote media through core DNS-pinned SSRF protection and gives Baileys bytes only; canonical `/api/media/<sha256>.<ext>` handles read directly through the registered content-addressed storage service with hard byte bounds
- enforces auth directories at 0700 and every credential/key file at 0600 after real Baileys writes, recursively rejecting symlinks and non-files
- reports connector health from the live per-account Baileys socket; a saved auth directory alone is never shown as connected in the frontend
- fails delivery when Baileys omits its authoritative message id or when any outbound attachment fails

## Architecture

- Personal/local or managed agent: bundled Baileys + frontend QR pairing
- User-owned WhatsApp Business account: hosted Meta Cloud API connector
- Shared eliza.app public number: Blooio

Baileys is an npm library bundled into the Eliza process. It is not separately installed third-party software from the user's perspective.

7a:Tda0,# ElizaOS Overall Project Summary: Aug 22, 2026

On August 22, 2026, the ElizaOS framework underwent significant hardening, focusing on memory management, security, and cloud infrastructure stability. The team successfully integrated new agent capabilities, including DoorDash and advanced coding-agent adapters, while simultaneously reinforcing system resilience through strict input validation and optimized CI/CD workflows.

## ✅ Completed Work

### Memory & Content Management
*   **Optimized Memory Efficiency:** Implemented `ContentReference`, `ReadSlice`, and `ReadView` contracts to enable progressive content access and fixed memory leaks via fail-closed defaults ([elizaos/eliza#24305](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24305), [elizaos/eliza#24433](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24433), [elizaos/eliza#24147](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24147), [elizaos/eliza#24137](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24137)).
*   **Enhanced Data Security:** Added atomic direct grant authority for document stores to improve access control ([elizaos/eliza#24300](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24300)).

### Cloud Infrastructure & Security
*   **Hardened Cloud Services:** Improved billing and identity management by converging login/gateway identity and enforcing request budgets and timeout boundaries ([elizaos/eliza#23998](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/23998), [elizaos/eliza#24324](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24324), [elizaos/eliza#24067](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24067), [elizaos/eliza#24053](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24053), [elizaos/eliza#24127](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24127)).
*   **Resilience Improvements:** Deployed dormant manifest-v3 backup catalog workers to ensure system stability ([elizaos/eliza#24080](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24080)).

### Agent Integrations
*   **Expanded Agent Capabilities:** Introduced a guarded DoorDash integration, added Kimi and Grok subscription adapters for coding workflows, and enabled Omarchy desktop plugin support ([elizaos/eliza#24059](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24059), [elizaos/eliza#24205](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24205), [elizaos/eliza#24399](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/24399)).
